QUESTION BANK ANDROID APP COMPLETE PROJECT DEVELOPMENT & DELIVERY PLAN
============================================================

Document version: 1.77 (CAA Editor edit-mode edge-case correction over v1.76, recorded in section 86. A pure helper check was compiled/run in isolation; Android Gradle compile, KSP, full unit tests, APK and emulator verification are NOT VERIFIED.) (v1.74 CAAP UI refresh in section 83; v1.73 UI compile fix; v1.72 historical CI/emulator green results do not cover v1.73+ source.)
Planning baseline: 16 November 2026
Latest package status: v1.77 is a source-only correction over v1.76. The Type_CQ Edit add action now excludes persisted labels marked for deletion until the database transaction commits; pending visible blocks are counted when checking whether another persisted block may be deleted. Four pure regression unit tests were added. Android Gradle tasks did not start because Gradle 8.14.3 is not cached and services.gradle.org DNS resolution failed; consequently Kotlin/Compose compile, KSP/Room generation, Gradle unit tests, instrumentation/emulator tests and APK assembly are NOT VERIFIED for v1.77. CAA parity remains PARTIAL, including one-row-per-label limitations, incomplete Chapter_CQ grouped editor, KaTeX, full math toolbar, image editing/viewer, solution marker compatibility and hierarchy/error-navigation work. Phase 0–16 statuses and their outstanding criteria remain as documented in the historical plan; Phase 17 remains NOT STARTED.
Historical foundation evidence (not evidence for the current source candidate): Single-database architecture confirmed; canonical SQL and Room build harness prepared. CI run 11 (package v0.2.8) passed: KSP/Room generation, unit tests (5 of 5) and debug APK assembly. The package is still a build harness, not a complete application; Room-database runtime behaviour (CHECK emulation, Bengali FTS on the real database) is not yet device-tested.

============================================================ 1. PROJECT
OBJECTIVE ============================================================

Build a complete, reliable, offline-first Android Question Bank
application.

The application will support:

-   Independent Type_CQ and Chapter_CQ systems.
-   Subject, chapter, topic and question-type hierarchy management.
-   Stimulus-based question groups.
-   Minimum Editor and Full Editor using one shared data model.
-   Question cards and question management.
-   Board and examination-year tagging.
-   Category management.
-   Ordered text/image solutions and mathematical content.
-   Image import, processing, integrity checking and lifecycle
    management.
-   Full-text search, filters and duplicate detection.
-   Question paper creation and immutable source snapshots.
-   Backup and restore.
-   Database migration and crash recovery.
-   Production Android builds and release testing.

The final application must be functional, persistent, recoverable,
testable and suitable for real-world use.

This plan uses the uploaded Room Database Implementation Blueprint as
the architectural baseline. It does not treat the existing ZIP build
harness as a completed application.

============================================================ 2. CURRENT
PROJECT BASELINE
============================================================

Known project materials:

1.  Canonical SQLite Schema v1.1.
2.  Room Entity/DAO Design Draft v1.
3.  Room Database Implementation Blueprint v1.
4.  Android build-harness ZIP.
5.  Feature-to-Schema Audit Matrix containing 146 numbered Editor
    behaviors.

Verified database baseline after direct inspection of the canonical SQL,
the Entity/DAO draft and the build-harness ZIP:

-   The canonical SQL declares 35 ordinary application tables.
-   The canonical SQL declares 2 FTS5 virtual tables.
-   SQLite creates 10 FTS5 shadow tables internally. These are not
    application tables and must not be mapped as Room Entities.
-   The resulting SQLite table catalogue contains 47 entries:
    35 ordinary tables + 2 FTS virtual tables + 10 FTS shadow tables.
-   The canonical SQL defines 17 triggers and 48 CHECK clauses.
-   The build harness contains 35 Room Entity files and registers 35
    entities. Its Database class currently exposes only a smoke-test DAO.
-   The build-harness callback creates the 2 FTS virtual tables and
    installs 6 FTS synchronization triggers. It does not currently
    install the other 11 canonical business-integrity triggers.
-   The Entity/DAO design draft contains 45 Entity declarations. Ten of
    those declarations map FTS shadow tables and must not be used as
    ordinary Room Entities. The remaining 35 correspond to the ordinary
    application tables.

The implementation blueprint's statement that there are 45 standard
SQLite tables conflicts with the canonical SQL. The canonical SQL must be
the authority for the table inventory. Correct the blueprint and publish
one reconciled inventory before implementation.

Type_CQ and Chapter_CQ have separate repository boundaries.
-   UI must not call DAO methods directly.
-   Minimum Editor and Full Editor share the same EditorDraft and save
    use case.
-   Editor persistence must be transactional.
-   File operations must be coordinated with database metadata through
    an operation journal.
-   All 146 numbered Editor behaviors must be traced to implementation
    and acceptance criteria.

Important distinction: The ZIP is a compatibility/build harness, not a
completed Android application. It has 35 Entity files, but its Database
class exposes only a smoke-test DAO. It is not evidence that the complete
DAO layer, repositories, UI features, migrations, media workflows or
release requirements are implemented. Its BUILD_STATUS file records
that no authoritative Gradle compile result was obtained.

A local SQLite execution of the canonical SQL returned integrity_check
= ok and no foreign_key_check violations. This is a useful SQL baseline,
not an Android build result and not proof of Room parity.

Before development proceeds, correct the blueprint's 45-standard-table
claim and publish a canonical inventory containing exactly 35 ordinary
tables, 2 FTS virtual tables, 10 SQLite-managed FTS shadow tables,
17 triggers and 48 CHECK clauses. Map only the 35 ordinary application
tables as Room Entities. Do not map FTS virtual or shadow tables as
ordinary Room Entities. Compare the canonical SQL, Room Entity mappings,
Room-generated schema and custom SQL objects directly.

============================================================ 3.
ARCHITECTURAL RULES — MUST NOT BE VIOLATED
============================================================

3.1 Application layering

UI -> ViewModel -> UseCase -> Repository -> DAO -> Room / SQLite

-   Activity, Fragment and Compose UI must not access DAOs directly.
-   ViewModels expose observable state and accept UI events.
-   UseCases coordinate domain operations.
-   Repositories enforce business rules and scope boundaries.
-   DAOs contain database operations and explicit SQL queries.

3.2 Scope separation

Type_CQ and Chapter_CQ must have separate:

-   Entity packages where their schemas differ.
-   DAO packages.
-   Repository interfaces and implementations.
-   Domain models where their hierarchy differs.
-   Validation rules.
-   Save and load operations.
-   Tests.

Shared infrastructure should be limited to genuinely shared concepts
such as board catalog, app metadata, media coordination, operation
journal and paper management.

Chapter_CQ must not accidentally access Type_CQ-specific Topic or
QuestionType tables. Type_CQ must not accidentally write Chapter_CQ
data.

3.3 Editor model

Minimum Editor and Full Editor must use the same EditorDraft and save
use case.

Changing editor mode must never: - Clear hidden fields. - Replace
existing values with defaults. - Drop images or relationships. - Remove
solution parts. - Change question identifiers. - Lose unsaved changes.

3.4 Database rules

-   Explicit ORDER BY for all user-visible ordered collections.
-   Flow for observable lists and suspend functions for one-shot
    operations.
-   Use typed projections and explicit JOIN queries where appropriate.
-   Use Room transactions for relational multi-step saves.
-   Never use destructive migration as a production fallback.
-   Export and version-control Room schema JSON.
-   Preserve canonical foreign-key and integrity rules.
-   Define one timestamp unit and use it consistently.
-   Keep file-system operations outside long-running Room transactions.

3.5 Data safety

-   No silent data loss.
-   No silent omission of missing or corrupt images.
-   No deletion of source questions that invalidates paper snapshots.
-   No destructive backup/restore operation while an editor save is
    active.
-   No release until migration, restore and recovery tests pass.

============================================================ 4. PROJECT
MODULE AND PACKAGE STRUCTURE
============================================================

Recommended structure:

app/ data/ local/ QuestionBankDatabase.kt entity/ shared/ typecq/
chaptercq/ paper/ dao/ shared/ typecq/ chaptercq/ paper/ migration/
callback/ repository/ TypeCqRepositoryImpl.kt ChapterCqRepositoryImpl.kt
PaperRepositoryImpl.kt CategoryRepositoryImpl.kt MediaRepositoryImpl.kt

domain/ model/ repository/ usecase/ validation/

feature/ home/ hierarchy/ editor/ common/ minimum/ full/ state/
validation/ questioncards/ category/ board/ search/ paper/ settings/

media/ ImagePipeline.kt ImageIntegrityChecker.kt
ImageReferenceResolver.kt OrphanMediaCollector.kt

backup/ BackupWorker.kt RestoreWorker.kt BackupManifest.kt

externaldb/ ExternalEditCoordinator.kt

core/ common/ database/ navigation/ ui/ testing/

Use dependency injection consistently. Hilt or another established
dependency-injection approach may be selected, but the project must use
one coherent approach rather than mixing service locators and ad hoc
construction.

============================================================ 5. COMPLETE
DEVELOPMENT PHASES
============================================================

PHASE 0 — REQUIREMENTS AND PROJECT CONTROL Priority: Critical

Objectives: - Establish a single authoritative project specification. -
Reconcile all uploaded design documents. - Freeze terminology and
feature scope. - Prevent implementation from silently changing database
behavior.

Tasks: 1. Inventory all project files and versions. 2. Identify the
canonical SQLite schema. 3. Identify the latest Entity/DAO draft. 4.
Identify all 146 Editor behavior requirements. 5. Correct the
implementation blueprint's table-count discrepancy using the canonical
SQL. 6. Reconcile 35 ordinary tables, 2 FTS virtual tables, 10 FTS
shadow tables, 17 triggers and 48 CHECK clauses. 7. Remove the 10 FTS
shadow-table declarations from the Room Entity mapping plan. 8. Compare
the 35 ordinary-table mappings with the 35 harness Entity files. 9.
Compare all canonical triggers with the build-harness callback and add
the missing 11 business-integrity triggers. 10. Reconcile indexes,
foreign keys, defaults, nullability and CHECK enforcement. 11. Create a
decision log for unresolved issues. 12. Create a feature-to-implementation
traceability matrix. 13. Define app navigation and screen inventory.
14. Define minimum supported Android version and target SDK policy.
15. Freeze naming conventions and package conventions.

Deliverables: - Project Requirements Specification v1. - Architecture
Decision Record (ADR) log. - Canonical database inventory. - 146-item
traceability spreadsheet. - Screen and navigation inventory. - Risk
register.

Exit criteria: - No unresolved ambiguity about Type_CQ versus
Chapter_CQ. - Every feature has an owner layer and acceptance
criterion. - Schema differences are documented and approved.

PHASE 1 — ANDROID BUILD FOUNDATION Priority: Critical

Objectives: - Turn the existing build harness into a reproducible
Android project foundation.

Tasks: 1. Open the ZIP in a clean Android Studio environment. 2. Verify
Gradle wrapper and plugin configuration. 3. Freeze Kotlin, Android
Gradle Plugin, Gradle, Room, KSP, Java and SDK compatibility. 4. Confirm
minSdk and targetSdk. 5. Establish debug and release build variants. 6.
Configure dependency management. 7. Configure dependency injection. 8.
Configure Kotlin coroutines and Flow. 9. Configure navigation and
Material UI foundation. 10. Configure unit-test and instrumentation-test
infrastructure. 11. Configure CI build workflow. 12. Ensure CI reports
compile and test results. 13. Preserve build artifacts and Room schema
exports.

Deliverables: - Reproducible Android Studio project. - Successful clean
debug build. - Successful CI build. - Unit and instrumentation test
configuration. - Version-controlled dependency and build configuration.

Exit criteria: - Clean checkout builds without manual undocumented
steps. - CI build is green and its Gradle compile/test logs are retained.
- The first successful CI run is treated as the authoritative build
result; do not describe the current harness as compiled until that run
passes. - No unresolved compiler or dependency errors.

PHASE 2 — DATABASE FOUNDATION AND ROOM PARITY Priority: Critical

Objectives: - Implement and verify the canonical database using Room.

Tasks: 1. Reconcile all standard SQLite tables against the Room mapping
inventory. 2. Confirm which tables are normal application tables and
which are FTS virtual or internal shadow tables. 3. Implement all
required Room Entities. 4. Implement complete DAO accessors in the
database class. 5. Implement primary keys and composite keys. 6.
Implement foreign keys and their delete/update actions. 7. Implement
unique indexes and normal indexes. 8. Verify default SQL expressions and
nullability. 9. Preserve CHECK constraints through supported schema
definitions or equivalent database enforcement. 10. Install canonical
triggers. 11. Install FTS5 virtual tables and synchronization triggers.
12. Configure database callbacks. 13. Enable and test foreign-key
enforcement. 14. Configure and device-test WAL policy. 15. Enable Room
schema export. 16. Store schema JSON in version control. 17. Compare
Room-generated schema with canonical SQL. 18. Add schema parity checks
to CI. 19. Add database integrity tests. 20. Add migration tests before
changing schema versions. 21. Test PRAGMA integrity_check and
foreign_key_check on created and migrated databases. 22. Verify FTS
initial population/rebuild as well as insert, update and delete
synchronization. 23. Verify all 17 canonical triggers and all 48 CHECK
constraints with positive and negative test cases.

Important review points: - The canonical SQL declares 35 ordinary
application tables and 2 FTS5 virtual tables; SQLite creates 10 FTS5
shadow tables internally. - The Entity/DAO draft's 45 declarations include
10 FTS shadow-table declarations; do not register those as Room Entities.
- The build harness's 35 Entity files align with the 35 ordinary tables,
but its Database class currently exposes only a smoke-test DAO. - The
canonical SQL defines 17 triggers. The harness callback installs only
the 6 FTS triggers; implement and test the other 11 canonical business
triggers as well. - The canonical SQL contains 48 CHECK clauses. Ensure
all are enforced in the actual database; UI validation alone is not a
replacement for database integrity enforcement.

Deliverables: - Complete Room Entity set. - Complete DAO accessor
inventory. - Room database class. - FTS setup and callbacks. - Canonical
trigger installation. - Schema JSON. - SQL-to-Room parity report. -
Database unit and instrumentation tests.

Exit criteria: - All expected application tables exist. - Foreign keys
and indexes match the approved schema. - Required constraints and
triggers are enforced. - FTS insert/update/delete tests pass. - Room
schema export is reviewed and committed. - No destructive migration
fallback is present.

PHASE 3 — DATABASE MIGRATION AND INITIAL DATA Priority: Critical

Objectives: - Make database creation, upgrade and seed data safe.

Tasks: 1. Define database version policy. 2. Separate database schema
version from application version. 3. Determine whether an existing v1
database must upgrade to the v1.1 schema. 4. Write explicit Room
Migration objects for every supported upgrade path. 5. Rebuild tables
where necessary. 6. Copy data in correct foreign-key order. 7. Migrate
board catalog data safely. 8. Recreate triggers and indexes. 9. Rebuild
FTS indexes. 10. Verify row counts and relationship consistency. 11.
Test migration with realistic populated databases. 12. Test interrupted
migration and recovery. 13. Define built-in board seed versioning. 14.
Define custom board archive and rename rules. 15. Define timestamp unit
and timezone convention.

Deliverables: - Migration design. - Migration implementation. -
Versioned seed data. - Migration test fixtures. - Data integrity report.

Exit criteria: - Existing data survives supported migrations. - No
migration path silently drops user data. - Foreign-key and FTS checks
pass after migration.

PHASE 4 — DOMAIN MODELS, DAO AND REPOSITORY LAYER Priority: Critical

Objectives: - Implement the full data-access and business-operation
layer.

Shared DAOs: - BoardCatalogDao. - AppMetadataDao. -
OperationJournalDao. - PaperCategoryDao. - PaperDao. -
PaperSectionDao. - PaperQuestionItemDao. - PaperImageDao. - Shared media
metadata DAOs.

Type_CQ DAOs: - SubjectDao. - ChapterDao. - TopicDao. -
QuestionTypeDao. - StimulusDao. - QuestionDao. - Board-link DAOs. -
Category-link DAOs. - Stimulus-image DAOs. - Solution-part DAOs.

Chapter_CQ DAOs: - SubjectDao or appropriately scoped subject access. -
ChapterDao. - StimulusDao. - QuestionDao. - Board-link DAOs. -
Category-link DAOs. - Stimulus-image DAOs. - Solution-part DAOs.

Repository implementations: - TypeCqRepositoryImpl. -
ChapterCqRepositoryImpl. - PaperRepositoryImpl. -
CategoryRepositoryImpl. - MediaRepositoryImpl.

UseCases: - SaveTypeCqEditor. - SaveChapterCqEditor. -
LoadEditorDraft. - ValidateEditorDraft. - AssignQuestionCategories. -
ReplaceBoardYearLinks. - ReorderSolutionParts. - BuildPaperSnapshot. -
CheckDuplicateQuestion.

Additional tasks: 1. Implement all observable queries using Flow. 2.
Implement one-shot queries using suspend. 3. Add explicit ordering to
every ordered query. 4. Implement transaction-safe relation replacement.
5. Implement typed editor projections. 6. Implement question-card
projections. 7. Enforce scope boundaries. 8. Add repository tests for
invalid references. 9. Add category archive/reassignment workflow. 10.
Add custom board policies.

Deliverables: - Complete DAO layer. - Repository interfaces and
implementations. - Domain models. - UseCases. - Repository tests.

Exit criteria: - No UI-to-DAO calls. - Type_CQ and Chapter_CQ isolation
tests pass. - All ordering and relationship rules are enforced.

PHASE 5 — HIERARCHY AND REFERENCE DATA UI Priority: High

Objectives: - Build the navigation and management foundation required by
the editor.

Features: 1. Subject list and management. 2. Chapter list and
management. 3. Topic list and management for Type_CQ. 4. Question Type
selection and management. 5. Dependent dropdown refresh. 6. Create and
edit hierarchy items. 7. Opening-path display. 8. Active and archived
board selection. 9. Board/year tagging. 10. Category management. 11.
Category reassignment before deletion. 12. Empty, loading and error
states.

Deliverables: - Hierarchy screens. - Reference-data management
screens. - Board selection components. - Category management screens. -
UI tests.

Exit criteria: - All valid hierarchy paths work. - Changing a parent
refreshes dependent selections safely. - Invalid references cannot be
saved.

PHASE 6 — EDITOR STATE ENGINE Priority: Critical

Objectives: - Build a reliable shared EditorDraft and ViewModel state
machine before implementing the full UI.

EditorDraft fields: - scope: TYPE_CQ / CHAPTER_CQ. -
existingStimulusId. - existingQuestionIds. - hierarchy identifiers. -
stimulus text. - ordered stimulus images. - stimulus board/year links. -
stimulus rating. - reference text. - ordered question blocks. - stable
question identifiers. - subquestion labels. - raw marks input. -
question text. - short answer. - question rating. - question board/year
links. - category identifiers. - ordered solution parts. - deleted
question identifiers. - editor mode: MINIMUM / FULL. - dirty,
validation, duplicate-warning and saving states.

Tasks: 1. Implement immutable or safely controlled draft state. 2.
Implement UI events. 3. Implement validation state. 4. Implement
dirty-state tracking. 5. Implement field-level errors. 6. Implement
scroll-to-error behavior. 7. Implement Save state and duplicate-tap
protection. 8. Implement Back and unsaved-change confirmation. 9. Decide
Undo/Redo architecture. 10. Decide durable draft recovery policy. 11.
Implement editor reset and reload. 12. Preserve hidden fields during
mode switching. 13. Test state restoration and process-death behavior.

Recommended decisions: - Use an in-memory command/state history for
ordinary Undo/Redo. - Use durable draft storage if unsaved drafts must
survive process death. - Keep editor mode as UI state rather than
question data. - Keep raw marks input as String until validation and
conversion. - Preserve decimal marks without integer truncation.

Deliverables: - EditorDraft. - EditorState. - EditorEvent. -
EditorViewModel. - Validation engine. - Undo/Redo policy. - Draft
recovery policy. - State-machine tests.

Exit criteria: - Mode switching loses no data. - Validation errors are
deterministic. - Repeated Save events cannot create duplicates. - Draft
recovery meets the approved requirement.

PHASE 7 — MINIMUM EDITOR Priority: Critical

Objectives: - Deliver the first usable question-entry workflow.

Features: 1. Editor header. 2. Close and Back handling. 3. Dirty
indicator. 4. Essential hierarchy selection. 5. Stimulus text. 6.
Essential image controls. 7. Question blocks. 8. Subquestion labels. 9.
Marks. 10. Question text. 11. Short answer. 12. Essential board/year
selection. 13. Essential category selection. 14. Save. 15. Validation
messages. 16. Unsaved-change confirmation. 17. Loading and error states.

Tasks: - Build responsive layouts. - Connect UI to ViewModel events. -
Implement keyboard behavior. - Implement focus management. - Implement
save and reload. - Implement create and edit flows.

Deliverables: - Functional Minimum Editor for Type_CQ. - Functional
Minimum Editor for Chapter_CQ. - UI automation tests.

Exit criteria: - A new question group can be created, saved, closed and
reloaded correctly in both scopes.

PHASE 8 — FULL EDITOR Priority: High

Objectives: - Implement the complete editing experience without
introducing a second data model.

Features: 1. All Minimum Editor controls. 2. Full stimulus metadata. 3.
Stimulus ratings. 4. Reference text. 5. Top/bottom image placement. 6.
Image ordering and replacement. 7. Full question metadata. 8. Multiple
board/year tags. 9. Multiple categories. 10. Ordered solution parts. 11.
Text and image solution blocks. 12. Mathematical input toolbar. 13.
Legacy solution-marker import. 14. Add, remove and reorder question
blocks. 15. Full preview. 16. Undo/Redo. 17. Full validation. 18. Save
and reload.

Deliverables: - Full Editor for Type_CQ. - Full Editor for Chapter_CQ. -
Ordered solution editor. - Preview components. - UI and state tests.

Exit criteria: - Every field survives mode switching, save, application
restart and reload. - All ordered content retains its exact order.

PHASE 9 — ATOMIC EDITOR PERSISTENCE Priority: Critical

Objectives: - Make multi-table question-group saves reliable.

Tasks: 1. Create immutable EditorSaveCommand. 2. Validate scope and
hierarchy IDs. 3. Lock Save state in ViewModel. 4. Execute relational
writes inside Room withTransaction. 5. Insert or update stimulus. 6.
Insert or update question blocks. 7. Remove explicitly deleted question
records. 8. Replace ordered solution parts. 9. Replace stimulus and
question board/year links. 10. Replace category links. 11. Verify
relationship consistency. 12. Commit transaction. 13. Emit success only
after commit. 14. Roll back all relational changes on failure. 15. Add
duplicate-save and forced-failure tests.

Deliverables: - Atomic save implementation. - Transaction tests. - Save
failure handling. - Duplicate prevention tests.

Exit criteria: - No partial relational save. - No duplicate group after
rapid repeated Save. - Existing question IDs remain stable during edits.

PHASE 10 — IMAGE AND MEDIA PIPELINE Priority: High

Objectives: - Build reliable image storage and ordered image/solution
relationships.

Features: 1. Android image picker. 2. Camera capture if included in
approved scope. 3. Crop. 4. Rotate. 5. Compression. 6. Decode and
readback verification. 7. SHA-256 integrity verification. 8. Stable
image identifiers. 9. TOP/BOTTOM stimulus placement. 10. Image reorder.
11. Replace image. 12. Remove image. 13. Image viewer. 14. Export. 15.
Missing-image state. 16. Corrupt-image state. 17. Memory-safe image
loading. 18. Orphan-media collection.

Operation sequence: 1. Write to temporary storage. 2. Decode and verify
the file. 3. Calculate SHA-256. 4. Create PENDING operation journal
entry. 5. Move file atomically to final location. 6. Commit database
metadata and ownership links. 7. Mark operation COMMITTED. 8. Recover
interrupted operations after process restart.

Tasks: - Implement ImagePipeline. - Implement ImageIntegrityChecker. -
Implement ImageReferenceResolver. - Implement OrphanMediaCollector. -
Implement recovery worker. - Prevent deletion while assets are
referenced. - Define paper snapshot image ownership. - Test low-memory
and process-death cases.

Deliverables: - Complete media subsystem. - Journal recovery. - Image
lifecycle tests. - Image integrity report.

Exit criteria: - No silent missing-image behavior. - Failed imports
leave no broken ownership records. - Referenced files are not deleted. -
Interrupted operations are recoverable.

PHASE 11 — QUESTION CARDS AND QUESTION MANAGEMENT Priority: High

Objectives: - Provide the primary browsing and management experience.

Features: 1. Question cards. 2. Stimulus-group display. 3. Subquestion
previews. 4. Hierarchy information. 5. Board/year chips. 6. Rating
display. 7. Category chips. 8. Image indicators. 9. Solution indicators.
10. Open editor. 11. Edit question. 12. Delete question with appropriate
confirmation. 13. Group-level and question-level operations. 14. Sorting
and pagination where necessary. 15. Empty and loading states.

Tasks: - Build card projections. - Implement list observation. -
Implement sorting and filtering. - Add Paging 3 if collection size
justifies it. - Test partial edits and deletes against legacy behavior.

Deliverables: - Question card screen. - Question detail screen. -
Question management actions. - UI tests.

Exit criteria: - Cards display accurate data. - Sorting and ordering are
deterministic. - Editing a card opens the correct scope and record.

PHASE 12 — SEARCH, FILTERS AND DUPLICATE DETECTION Priority: High

Objectives: - Provide fast and accurate discovery of questions.

Features: 1. FTS5 full-text search. 2. Bengali text search. 3. English
text search. 4. Normalized search input. 5. Subject filter. 6. Chapter
filter. 7. Topic filter for Type_CQ. 8. Question Type filter for
Type_CQ. 9. Category filter. 10. Board filter. 11. Examination-year
filter. 12. Rating filter. 13. Scope filter. 14. Sorting. 15. Duplicate
warning. 16. Search result highlighting where appropriate.

Tasks: - Define Unicode normalization policy. - Test punctuation and
whitespace handling. - Test Bengali and English tokenization. - Verify
insert/update/delete synchronization. - Test FTS rebuild after
migration. - Define fallback behavior if a supported SQLite
configuration cannot provide FTS5. - Implement duplicate candidate
comparison. - Avoid treating a text-similarity warning as proof that two
questions are identical.

Deliverables: - Search repository methods. - Search UI. - Filter UI. -
FTS tests. - Duplicate detection tests.

Exit criteria: - Search reflects the latest saved content. - Old search
terms disappear after updates. - Deleted records are not returned. -
Filters combine correctly.

PHASE 13 — PAPER BUILDER Priority: High

Objectives: - Allow users to create question papers from both question
scopes.

Features: 1. Paper creation. 2. Paper title and metadata. 3. Paper
category. 4. Ordered sections. 5. Add source question from Type_CQ. 6.
Add source question from Chapter_CQ. 7. Snapshot question content. 8.
Snapshot solution and media references as required. 9. Per-paper item
overrides. 10. Reorder sections. 11. Reorder questions. 12. Marks and
totals. 13. Validate paper. 14. Preview paper. 15. Export/print if
approved. 16. Preserve paper content after source-question deletion.

Important design decisions: - Paper source is polymorphic and must be
resolved through separate scope-specific queries or DTOs. - Snapshot
JSON must have a schemaVersion. - Snapshot migration and validation
codecs are required. - Define whether paper images are copied assets or
independently retained references. - Source deletion must not make a
saved paper unreadable.

Deliverables: - Paper data layer. - Paper editor. - Paper preview. -
Snapshot codec. - Paper integrity tests.

Exit criteria: - Papers can be created from both scopes. - Section and
item ordering survives reload. - Source edits or deletion do not
unexpectedly rewrite existing snapshots. - Paper snapshots remain
readable after application upgrades.

PHASE 14 — BACKUP AND RESTORE Priority: Critical

Objectives: - Protect user data and provide reliable transfer/recovery.

Features: 1. Manual backup. 2. Automatic backup policy if approved. 3.
Backup manifest. 4. Database backup. 5. Media backup. 6. Schema/version
metadata. 7. Integrity hashes. 8. Restore preview. 9. Restore
validation. 10. Safe restore. 11. Conflict handling. 12. Interrupted
restore recovery.

Tasks: - Define backup format. - Define manifest schema. - Include
application and database version. - Include media inventory and
hashes. - Verify archive contents before restore. - Prevent editor
writes during destructive restore. - Restore into a staging location
first. - Verify integrity before switching to restored data. - Preserve
or roll back the prior database if restore fails. - Test incomplete
archives and corrupted media.

Deliverables: - BackupWorker. - RestoreWorker. - BackupManifest. -
Backup/restore UI. - Recovery tests.

Exit criteria: - Backup can be verified. - Restore does not partially
overwrite the active dataset. - Database and media remain consistent. -
Failed restore leaves the previous valid data recoverable.

PHASE 15 — SETTINGS, ACCESSIBILITY AND POLISH Priority: Medium

Features: 1. Editor preferences. 2. Image settings. 3. Default editor
mode. 4. Search preferences. 5. Theme settings if approved. 6.
Responsive layouts. 7. Keyboard navigation. 8. Touch targets. 9.
Screen-reader labels. 10. Focus order. 11. Dialog and snackbar behavior.
12. Error-state presentation. 13. Performance and memory improvements.

Tasks: - Define app_metadata keys and defaults. - Avoid arbitrary
database columns for preferences. - Test on small and large screens. -
Test keyboard and touch interactions. - Test accessibility semantics. -
Profile image loading and large question lists.

Deliverables: - Settings screen. - Accessibility report. - UI polish
report. - Performance report.

PHASE 16 — SYSTEM INTEGRATION AND REGRESSION Priority: Critical

Objectives: - Verify the complete application as one integrated system.

Tasks: 1. Run all unit tests. 2. Run all Room tests. 3. Run all
migration tests. 4. Run all repository tests. 5. Run all editor tests.
6. Run all media tests. 7. Run all search tests. 8. Run all paper tests.
9. Run backup/restore tests. 10. Run UI automation tests. 11. Run
process-death recovery tests. 12. Run low-memory tests. 13. Run
large-dataset tests. 14. Run cross-scope isolation tests. 15. Run the
complete 146-item Editor audit. 16. Verify every requirement maps to a
test or explicit UI-only criterion. 17. Verify no critical unresolved
issue remains.

Deliverables: - Full regression report. - 146-item traceability
completion report. - Defect list and resolution record. - Release
candidate build.

Exit criteria: - All critical and high-priority tests pass. - No known
data-loss defect. - No known cross-scope data corruption. - No
unresolved critical migration issue.

PHASE 17 — RELEASE AND DELIVERY Priority: Critical

Objectives: - Deliver a reproducible, signed and documented Android
application.

Tasks: 1. Freeze release version. 2. Review permissions. 3. Review
application ID and signing configuration. 4. Configure release build. 5.
Remove debug-only behavior. 6. Verify release database configuration. 7.
Run release APK installation test. 8. Test upgrade from previous
supported version. 9. Test backup and restore on release build. 10.
Generate checksums. 11. Prepare release notes. 12. Prepare user guide.
13. Prepare developer documentation. 14. Archive source and release
artifacts.

Deliverables: - Signed release APK. - AAB if distribution requires it. -
Release notes. - Installation guide. - User guide. - Developer handover
document. - Test report. - Versioned source and schema artifacts.

Exit criteria: - Release APK installs and runs. - Database and media
tests pass on release build. - Signing and versioning are verified. -
All release deliverables are archived.

============================================================ 7. PROJECT
ACCEPTANCE CRITERIA
============================================================

The project is complete only when all of the following are true:

1.  The Android application builds reproducibly from a clean checkout.
2.  The approved canonical database is implemented and verified.
3.  Room schema parity is reviewed.
4.  Database integrity rules are enforced.
5.  Type_CQ and Chapter_CQ work independently.
6.  Minimum Editor and Full Editor use the same draft model.
7.  All required editor behaviors work.
8.  Save, update and delete operations are transaction-safe.
9.  Images remain consistent with database ownership records.
10. Search and filters return current results.
11. Question cards show correct data.
12. Paper snapshots survive source-question deletion.
13. Backup and restore preserve database and media integrity.
14. Migrations preserve existing user data.
15. All 146 numbered Editor behaviors have traceability.
16. Critical regression tests pass.
17. Release APK is signed and installation-tested.
18. User and developer documentation is complete.

A source file existing is not proof that a feature works. A passing test
or documented, approved UI-only acceptance criterion is required.

============================================================ 8. RISKS
AND MITIGATION
============================================================

RISK 1: Room schema differs from canonical SQL. Mitigation: Automated
schema comparison and database tests before feature development.

RISK 2: Missing constraints or triggers allow inconsistent data.
Mitigation: Preserve database-level enforcement and test invalid writes.

RISK 3: Type_CQ and Chapter_CQ become coupled. Mitigation: Separate
repositories, DAOs, domain models and scope tests.

RISK 4: Editor mode switching loses hidden data. Mitigation: One
EditorDraft and shared save operation; round-trip tests.

RISK 5: Image file and database become inconsistent after a crash.
Mitigation: Operation journal, temporary files, atomic rename and
recovery.

RISK 6: Migration destroys existing questions. Mitigation: Explicit
migrations, realistic fixtures and backup verification.

RISK 7: Paper becomes unreadable after source deletion. Mitigation:
Versioned snapshots and independent asset-lifecycle policy.

RISK 8: FTS results become stale. Mitigation: Trigger lifecycle tests
and index rebuild after migration.

RISK 9: Large datasets cause slow cards or search. Mitigation: Query
profiling, indexes, pagination and performance tests.

RISK 10: Feature scope expands without traceability. Mitigation:
Maintain the 146-item audit and formal change log.

MANAGEMENT AND DEVELOPMENT ORDER
============================================================

Recommended execution order:

1.  Requirements and architecture freeze.
2.  Android build foundation.
3.  Database parity and constraints.
4.  Migration and seed data.
5.  DAO and repository layer.

18. Release.

Do not begin the complete editor UI while the database contract remains
unstable.

Each phase must have: - A defined owner. - A task list. - A
deliverable. - Tests. - Exit criteria. - A recorded status.

Recommended status values: NOT STARTED IN PROGRESS BLOCKED IN REVIEW
TESTING PASSED COMPLETED

============================================================ 12. SOURCE REVIEW CORRECTIONS — VERSION 1.1
============================================================

The following corrections are mandatory before implementation begins:

1. Canonical table inventory:
   - 35 ordinary application tables.
   - 2 FTS5 virtual tables.
   - 10 FTS5 shadow tables generated by SQLite.
   - 47 total table entries in sqlite_master when the FTS objects exist.
   The phrase "45 standard SQLite tables" in the implementation
   blueprint is inconsistent with the canonical SQL and must be corrected.

2. Room Entity inventory:
   - The Entity/DAO draft contains 45 Entity declarations.
   - Ten declarations represent FTS shadow tables and must be removed
     from the Room Entity registration design.
   - The 35 ordinary application tables are the Room Entity mapping
     target, subject to field-by-field parity review.

3. Trigger coverage:
   - Canonical SQL: 17 triggers.
   - Build-harness callback: 6 FTS triggers.
   - Missing from the callback: 11 non-FTS business-integrity triggers:
     trg_chapter_question_chapter_match_insert,
     trg_chapter_question_chapter_match_update,
     trg_chapter_stimulus_chapter_parent_update,
     trg_paper_item_section_match_insert,
     trg_paper_item_section_match_update,
     trg_paper_item_source_required_insert,
     trg_paper_section_parent_update,
     trg_system_paper_category_no_delete,
     trg_type_question_topic_match_insert,
     trg_type_question_topic_match_update,
     trg_type_stimulus_topic_parent_update.
   All 17 must be installed through an approved, idempotent database
   initialization/migration strategy and tested.

4. CHECK enforcement:
   - Canonical SQL contains 48 CHECK clauses.
   - Every clause must be accounted for in a parity matrix and enforced
     by the database, not merely by UI validation.

5. Build evidence:
   - The ZIP is a scaffold/build harness, not a completed app.
   - Its Database class exposes only a smoke-test DAO.
   - BUILD_STATUS reports that no authoritative Gradle compile result
     was available when the ZIP was authored.
   - A clean CI build and test run is a Phase 1 exit requirement.

6. SQL validation evidence:
   - Direct SQLite execution of the canonical SQL returned integrity_check
     = ok and no foreign_key_check violations in the inspected baseline.
   - This does not prove Room parity, Android compilation, migration
     correctness or runtime behavior.

7. Remaining design decisions:
   - Undo/Redo coverage and history policy.
   - Whether unsaved drafts must survive process death.
   - Paper snapshot image ownership and retention.
   - Custom board archive/rename behavior.
   - Timestamp unit and timezone policy.
   - External edit/backup/restore mutual exclusion.
   These must be recorded as decisions with acceptance tests, not silently
   assumed by implementation.

============================================================ 13. FINAL
PROJECT RULE
============================================================

The Question Bank project must be developed as a complete, integrated
application, not as a collection of isolated code files.

The database is the foundation. The repository layer protects the
domain. The EditorDraft protects editing state. The operation journal
protects media consistency. The paper snapshot protects historical
content. The migration and backup systems protect user data. The
acceptance tests protect the final product.

No phase is complete until its deliverables exist and its exit criteria
pass.

FINAL TARGET: A fully functional, offline-first, maintainable, tested
and release-ready Question Bank Android application supporting both
Type_CQ and Chapter_CQ, with complete editing, media, search,
paper-building and data-protection workflows.

END OF DOCUMENT


============================================================ 14. ARCHITECTURE DECISION UPDATE — VERSION 1.2
============================================================

ADR-001 — ONE PHYSICAL SQLITE DATABASE
Status: ACCEPTED
Decision date: 16 November 2026

The application will use one physical SQLite database file named
`QuestionBank.db`. Do not split Type_CQ, Chapter_CQ, or category data into
separate database files.

Logical separation remains mandatory:
- Type_CQ and Chapter_CQ retain separate table families, DAO boundaries,
  repository boundaries, and use cases.
- Paper-building tables remain in the same database because paper items
  can reference either question source.
- The shared board catalog and operation journal remain in the same
  database as specified by the canonical schema.
- Category tables remain exactly as defined in canonical schema v1.1:
  `type_cq_question_categories`, `chapter_cq_question_categories`, and
  `paper_categories`. This decision selects one physical database; it
  does not merge category semantics or alter the canonical schema.

Consequences:
1. Cross-domain references and paper-source relationships remain within
   one SQLite database boundary.
2. Related relational writes can use one database transaction.
3. Backup, restore, migration, and integrity checks use one database
   boundary.
4. Type_CQ and Chapter_CQ separation is enforced through domain, DAO,
   repository, and UI boundaries rather than separate database files.
5. Any future proposal to merge category tables or redesign category
   ownership requires a separate reviewed architecture decision and a
   migration plan.

15. PROJECT STATUS UPDATE — VERSION 1.3
============================================================

Planning update date: 16 November 2026
Package baseline: QuestionBank Android v0.2.3
Previous package: v0.2.2 KAPT metadata compatibility attempt

15.1 New CI evidence reviewed

The uploaded GitHub Actions diagnostic report identifies:
- Workflow run number: 6.
- Commit: 478388d46ef740ba74a9fb29c9445c27d6072c07.
- Java runtime: 17.0.21.
- Gradle task failure: `:app:kaptDebugKotlin`.
- Gradle process exit code: 1.
- Root diagnostic: Kotlin metadata version 2.4.0 was encountered, while
  the metadata reader selected on the annotation processor classpath
  reported a maximum supported version of 2.3.0.
- The stack trace enters Room's Kotlin metadata processing path
  (`KmClassContainer` / `KotlinClassMetadata.readStrict`).

This evidence points to annotation-processor metadata-reader
compatibility. It is not evidence of an Android SDK license failure in
that run. The JNI stripping warning and Gradle deprecation warning were
not the fatal error shown in the supplied log.

15.2 Package v0.2.2 change and v0.2.3 packaging update

Package v0.2.2 introduced the targeted dependency-alignment attempt:
`kapt("org.jetbrains.kotlin:kotlin-metadata-jvm:2.4.21")`.

Package v0.2.3:
- Carries forward that KAPT processor-classpath dependency.
- Updates the Android `versionName` to
  `0.2.3-database-foundation` so package and app version metadata agree.
- Includes this project plan as `PROJECT_PLAN_v1_3_UPDATED.txt`.
- Preserves the GitHub Actions workflow that captures the complete
  Gradle log and uploads diagnostics even when the build fails.
- Does not change the accepted one-physical-database architecture.
- Does not claim that the KAPT compatibility issue is resolved.

The dependency override is a testable compatibility hypothesis, not a
verified fix. The local packaging environment did not run Gradle and
cannot establish the resolved KAPT dependency graph or Android build
result.

15.3 Current phase status

PHASE 0 — REQUIREMENTS AND PROJECT CONTROL:
Status: IN REVIEW / BASELINE ESTABLISHED
Evidence: canonical schema inventory and architecture rules are
documented; the 146-item behavior traceability work and remaining
decision records still need completion.

PHASE 1 — ANDROID BUILD FOUNDATION:
Status: IN PROGRESS / BLOCKED ON CI VERIFICATION
Evidence: Android project harness, Gradle configuration and diagnostic
workflow are present. CI run 6 failed at `:app:kaptDebugKotlin`.
Exit criteria are not met until a clean CI run completes tests and
debug APK assembly and publishes the expected APK artifact.

PHASE 2 — DATABASE FOUNDATION AND ROOM PARITY:
Status: PARTIAL FOUNDATION ONLY
Evidence: 35 ordinary Room entity files, canonical SQL, database object
installation code, and a canonical SQL contract test are present in
the harness. The Database class and DAO surface are not yet the complete
application data layer. Full Room-generated schema parity, all DAO
accessors, migrations and comprehensive runtime verification remain
open.

PHASES 3–17:
Status: NOT STARTED unless explicitly evidenced by a later test report
or implementation review. Presence of a design note or scaffold file
does not count as feature completion.

15.4 Immediate next execution gate

1. Extract package v0.2.3 and push its project contents to the intended
   GitHub repository.
2. Run the `Android Test and APK Build` workflow.
3. Download the `QuestionBank-ci-report` artifact regardless of result.
4. On failure, inspect `gradle-build.log`, `gradle-exit-code.txt`, and
   generated Gradle/JUnit reports. Verify whether the same metadata
   parser error remains and inspect the resolved KAPT processor
   dependency graph before making another dependency change.

   both completed and that `QuestionBank-debug-apk` contains
   `app-debug.apk`.
6. Only then mark Phase 1's build gate as passed. A green workflow is
   necessary but does not prove the full application's feature
   completeness or Room schema parity.

If the direct metadata dependency does not resolve the parser mismatch,
evaluate the Room/Kotlin processor compatibility matrix and consider a
controlled KSP migration. Do not stack additional unverified dependency
overrides.

15.5 Package verification boundaries

The v0.2.3 ZIP may be checked for archive integrity, expected files,
source-level configuration and preservation of CI workflow contents.
These checks do not substitute for:
- Gradle dependency resolution.
- Kotlin/KAPT annotation processing.
- Android compilation.
- Unit-test execution.
- APK assembly or installation.
- Device/runtime database tests.

All status changes must cite a concrete CI report, test result, or code
review. Never mark a phase PASSED merely because source files exist.

- Gradle process exit code: 1.
- Failed Gradle task: `:app:kaptDebugKotlin`.

The prior Kotlin metadata parser exception (metadata 2.4.0 versus
reader maximum 2.3.0) is not present in this run's fatal diagnostics.
Gradle downloaded `kotlin-metadata-jvm:2.4.21`, and Room proceeded to
annotation processing.

The new reported Room diagnostics are:
- `QuestionBankDatabase`: `@Database annotation must specify list of
  entities`.
- `RoomSmokeDao`: no such table `type_cq_subjects`.
- `RoomSmokeDao`: no such table `chapter_cq_subjects`.
- Room-generated `QuestionBankDatabase_Impl.java`: invalid empty-array
  expressions reported as `illegal start of expression`.

Source review of package v0.2.3 confirms:
- 35 Kotlin entity source files are present.
- `QuestionBankDatabase.kt` contains a non-empty `entities = [...]`
  declaration referencing those entity classes.
- `RoomSmokeDao.kt` queries the two subject tables named above.

The uploaded CI artifact did not include the actual generated KAPT stub
source file. Therefore, the precise internal reason why the KAPT
processor received an empty entity list cannot be confirmed from the
available log alone. Treat the empty-list diagnostic as the observed
failure, not as proof that the Kotlin database source omitted entities.

16.2 Processor migration decision

Decision: migrate the Room compiler from KAPT to KSP.

Rationale:
- This project contains Kotlin source and Kotlin Room entity classes.
- Room 2.8.5's official setup guidance directs Kotlin-source projects
  to use KSP.
- KSP processes Kotlin symbols directly and avoids the KAPT stub
  generation path implicated by the current diagnostic pattern.
- Kotlin's KSP setup documentation currently demonstrates Kotlin
  2.4.21 with KSP plugin 2.3.12.

This is an implementation correction to the annotation-processing
path. It does not change the database schema, table families, entity
definitions, Room version, or accepted one-physical-database
architecture.

16.3 Package v0.2.4 changes

Package v0.2.4:
- Replaces `org.jetbrains.kotlin.kapt` with
  `com.google.devtools.ksp` version `2.3.12`.
- Moves Room compiler from `kapt(...)` to `ksp(...)`.
- Removes the `kotlin-metadata-jvm:2.4.21` KAPT classpath override.
- Moves Room schema and incremental processor options into the KSP
  configuration block.
- Sets Android `versionName` to
  `0.2.4-ksp-room-processing`.
- Preserves Room runtime version 2.8.5 and the existing 35 entities.
- Preserves the GitHub Actions diagnostic artifact workflow.
- Does not alter the single `QuestionBank.db` architecture or
  canonical SQL.

16.4 Updated phase status

PHASE 1 — ANDROID BUILD FOUNDATION:
Status: IN PROGRESS / BLOCKED ON CI VERIFICATION.
Run 7 shows that the previous metadata exception no longer appears,
but the Room KAPT processing task failed with an empty entity list.
Package v0.2.4 changes the processor path to KSP. Phase 1 remains
unpassed until CI completes tests and APK assembly.

PHASE 2 — DATABASE FOUNDATION AND ROOM PARITY:
Status: PARTIAL FOUNDATION ONLY.
The 35 entity source files and non-empty database entity declaration

17. CI RUN 8 REVIEW — ROOM ENTITY CLASS-LITERAL CORRECTION
============================================================

Planning update date: 16 November 2026
Package baseline: QuestionBank Android v0.2.5
Previous package: v0.2.4 KSP Room processing migration

17.1 CI run 8 evidence

The newly uploaded GitHub Actions report identifies:
- Workflow: Android Test and APK Build.
- Run number: 8.
- Run ID: 43882225061.
- Commit: 9a00d957b571a3beb9f5bb2539e2e334b4940b02.
- Java runtime: 17.0.21.
- Gradle process exit code: 1.
- Failed Gradle task: `:app:kspDebugKotlin`.

KSP starts successfully and loads the Room KSP provider:
`androidx.room.RoomKspProcessor$Provider`.

The reported processor diagnostics are:
- `QuestionBankDatabase.kt:53`: `@Database annotation must specify
  list of entities`.
- `RoomSmokeDao.kt`: tables `type_cq_subjects` and
  `chapter_cq_subjects` do not exist in Room's model.
- The DAO query results then cannot be mapped to `kotlin.Long`.

The preceding KAPT metadata parser exception is not reported in this
run. The current failure is now inside Room's database model construction
under KSP.

17.2 Confirmed source defect

Inspection of the v0.2.4 source archive shows that the `@Database`
annotation lists 35 entity identifiers as bare names, for example:

`entities = [TypeCqSubjectsEntity, ...]`

Kotlin annotation arguments for Room entity declarations must be
Kotlin class literals. The corrected form is:

`entities = [TypeCqSubjectsEntity::class, ...]`

This was a concrete source defect, not a KSP version incompatibility.
The Room KSP provider loaded and reported that it could not find the
entity class list because the entries were not class literals.

17.3 Package v0.2.5 changes

Package v0.2.5:
- Retains Kotlin 2.4.21, KSP 2.3.12 and Room 2.8.5.
- Retains KSP processing and removes no further build plugins.
- Changes all 35 entries in `QuestionBankDatabase.kt` to `EntityName::class`.
- Replaces the wildcard entity package import with explicit imports
  for the 35 entity classes.
- Adds a package/source verification assertion confirming that all
  35 expected entity class literals appear in the database annotation
  in canonical order.
- Sets Android `versionName` to
  `0.2.5-room-entity-class-literals`.
- Preserves the GitHub Actions diagnostic workflow.
- Does not alter the canonical SQL, entity definitions, table names,
  database version, or the one-physical-database architecture.

17.4 Updated phase status

PHASE 1 — ANDROID BUILD FOUNDATION:
Status: IN PROGRESS / BLOCKED ON CI VERIFICATION.
Run 8 confirms the KSP Room provider loads, but Room rejected the
entity declaration. Package v0.2.5 corrects the source annotation.
The build gate is not passed until the next CI run completes unit tests
and debug APK assembly.

PHASE 2 — DATABASE FOUNDATION AND ROOM PARITY:
Status: PARTIAL FOUNDATION ONLY.
35 entity source files are retained and are now referenced through
explicit class literals in `@Database`. Room-generated schema,
generated database implementation, canonical SQL parity, migration
coverage and runtime verification remain unverified.

17.5 Next CI verification gate

1. Extract v0.2.5 and push the project to the intended GitHub repository.

   the `@Database annotation must specify list of entities` error.

============================================================
18. INDEPENDENT REVIEW AND PACKAGE v0.2.6
============================================================

Planning update date: 17 November 2026
Package baseline: QuestionBank Android v0.2.6
Previous package: v0.2.5 Room entity class-literal correction

18.1 Review method and limits

An independent review of v0.2.5 (report: Fix_txt_file_by_clude.txt) was
re-checked against the source archive. Confirmed by direct inspection:
CHECK clauses absent from every Room Entity, no Gradle wrapper, schema JSON
git-ignored, duplicate SQL copy, FTS update trigger ignoring question_id,
redundant unique index on type_cq_question_category_links, trigger tests
that only check names, and Bengali word splitting in the default FTS5
tokenizer (reproduced with SQLite 3.45.1).
Not verified: any Gradle/Kotlin compilation, Room-generated database
behaviour, BundledSQLiteDriver behaviour, and the compatibility of AGP
8.13.2 + Kotlin 2.4.21 + Room 2.8.5 + androidx.sqlite 2.7.1.

18.2 Corrections to earlier statements in this plan

- Sections 2, 11 and 12 state that the harness callback installs only the
  6 FTS triggers and omits 11 business triggers. This is stale: since the
  v0.2.x Kotlin installer, CanonicalDatabaseObjects installs all 17
  canonical triggers plus the 2 FTS tables and the system category seed.
- The statement that CHECK clauses are enforced "through supported schema
  definitions" is not possible: Room @Entity cannot declare CHECK. Room
  creates tables itself, so the 48 CHECK clauses were absent from the real
  database in v0.2.5.

18.3 Package v0.2.6 changes

1. CHECK enforcement (decision): the 48 canonical CHECK clauses are kept in
   the canonical SQL and re-created in the Room database by 46 generated
   BEFORE INSERT / BEFORE UPDATE triggers (2 per table, 23 tables) in
   CheckEmulationTriggers.kt. Semantics match CHECK: a row is rejected only
   when an expression is false; NULL passes. Offline fuzz comparison
   (about 124,000 insert/update operations, SQLite 3.45.1) showed 0 differences
   from the real CHECK constraints. Repository-level validation is still
   required in addition; database enforcement is the last line of defence.
   Runtime trigger count: 17 canonical + 46 CHECK-emulation = 63.
2. Bengali FTS (decision): both FTS5 tables use
   unicode61 with tokenchars containing the Bengali vowel signs, virama,
   anusvara, visarga, candrabindu and nukta, so words are no longer split
   at vowel signs. Prefix queries (for example "pori*") still work.
   Substring search is not supported; trigram remains an alternative.
   Existing devices keep old FTS tables (IF NOT EXISTS); acceptable because
   the app is unpublished. Any later tokenizer change needs drop, recreate
   and full reindex in a migration.
3. FTS update triggers now fire on UPDATE OF question_id, question_text,
   short_answer for both FTS tables.
4. .gitignore no longer excludes app/schemas; Room schema JSON must be
   committed after the first successful build.
5. Redundant unique index removed from TypeCqQuestionCategoryLinksEntity.
6. The duplicate SQL copy under app/src/test/resources was removed; unit
   tests read the single canonical file in database/ via test sourceSets.
7. Tests: trigger-count assertion updated to 17 + 46; new tests for CHECK
   emulation (CHECK-stripped schema plus installer) and Bengali FTS.
8. Android versionName: 0.2.6-check-triggers-fts-tokenizer.

18.4 Updated phase status

PHASE 1 - ANDROID BUILD FOUNDATION: IN PROGRESS / BLOCKED ON CI
VERIFICATION. The v0.2.6 Kotlin and test code is uncompiled. The Gradle
wrapper is still missing.
PHASE 2 - DATABASE FOUNDATION AND ROOM PARITY: PARTIAL FOUNDATION ONLY.
CHECK emulation and Bengali tokenizer are implemented but not verified on
the Room-created database or with BundledSQLiteDriver. Room-schema versus
canonical-SQL parity, migration tests and an androidTest remain open.

18.5 Open work items from the review (priority order)

1. Add Gradle wrapper: run `gradle wrapper --gradle-version 8.14.3`, commit
   gradlew, gradlew.bat, gradle/wrapper/*; switch the workflow to ./gradlew.
2. Run CI; if it fails, use the first "e:" error in gradle-build.log.
   Suspects if dependency resolution fails: forced androidx.sqlite 2.7.1
   against Room 2.8.5, or the KSP/Kotlin/AGP combination.
3. After the first green build commit app/schemas/.../1.json and upload
   app/schemas/** as a CI artifact.
4. Add androidTest: Room in-memory database with BundledSQLiteDriver and the
   callback; valid insert; invalid inserts (CHECK emulation); Bengali FTS
   search; PRAGMA foreign_keys and journal_mode checks.
5. Add a schema parity test (Room schema JSON versus canonical SQL).
6. Set WAL and foreign-key policy explicitly in the database builder.
7. Canonical-object versioning: keep IF NOT EXISTS but define DROP + CREATE
   in migrations when a trigger body changes, or store
   canonical_objects_version in app_metadata.
8. Measure FTS delete/update cost on realistic data; if slow, key FTS rows
   by rowid.
9. Index names: Room generates index_<table>_<cols>; do not rely on the
   SQL idx_*/uq_* names in code or scripts.
10. Launcher icon: transparent foreground in all densities or vector.
11. Increase versionCode for every release.
12. Workflow: add concurrency (cancel-in-progress) and decide Gradle cache
    provider.
13. Documentation clean-up: README_BN.md (still v0.2.0/KAPT),
    KAPT_METADATA_COMPATIBILITY.md (historical), missing notes referenced
    by VERSION_NOTES_0.2.3, ZIP/folder naming mismatch.
14. Seed data (board_catalog, question types) stays planned under built-in
    board seed versioning; not a defect.

18.6 Next CI verification gate

- Failed task: :app:kspDebugKotlin (KSP PROCESSING_ERROR); build failed in 27 s.

  index_chapter_cq_solution_parts_question_id_position
  (ChapterCqSolutionPartsEntity.kt:12) and
  index_type_cq_solution_parts_question_id_position
  (TypeCqSolutionPartsEntity.kt:12).
- Progress: Room now recognises the entities (the run 8 error is gone).

19.2 Cause and fix (v0.2.7)

Both entities declared Index(question_id, position) twice, once non-unique
and once unique, which Room names identically. Only the unique index is kept
(it also serves ordered lookups). An automated scan of all 35 entities found
no other duplicate Room index names. The canonical SQL is unchanged and still
holds the redundant idx_*_solution_parts_order index; this is a documented,
harmless difference for the future schema parity test. versionName:
0.2.7-room-duplicate-index-fix.

============================================================
27. PACKAGE v0.2.14 - SCALE DECISIONS (HUNDREDS OF THOUSANDS OF QUESTIONS)
============================================================

Planning update date: 17 November 2026
Package baseline: QuestionBank Android v0.2.14

27.1 Requirement stated by the owner
- Several hundred thousand questions, each with solutions and images. The owner delegated the
  open design decisions (redundant indexes, FK indexes, WAL) to the assistant.

27.2 Decisions
- CHECK 48 vs 46 is NOT a discrepancy: 23 tables carry 48 CHECK clauses; the emulation uses
  2 triggers (insert, update) per table = 46. Removed from the open list.
- Redundant plain indexes on *_solution_parts removed from the canonical SQL.
- FK indexes: the earlier count of 11 uncovered foreign keys was wrong; there were 12. All 12 now
  have covering indexes, because at this scale deleting or updating a parent row (question, image,
  paper, section) must not scan whole child tables. Cost: slower bulk import and larger file;
  bulk import must run in a single transaction.
- WAL: journal mode set explicitly to WRITE_AHEAD_LOGGING. Backup/export must checkpoint first
  (PRAGMA wal_checkpoint(TRUNCATE)) or copy the -wal and -shm files together with the .db file.
- Images stay as files referenced by relative_path (never BLOBs in the database).
- Database version stays 1 until a first release; after any released install, every schema change
  needs a Room migration and a new schema JSON.

27.3 Risks to measure before building features (add as a benchmark test/android test)
- Import speed of several hundred thousand questions with 46 CHECK-emulation triggers and
  FTS triggers active (consider batching and measuring).
- FTS5 index size and Bangla search speed at 300,000+ rows.
- Database file size and backup/export time with WAL.
- Image folder size and sync/backup strategy (folder with relative_path).

27.4 Status
PHASE 1: DONE except documenting the dependency/version freeze.
PHASE 2: PARTIAL FOUNDATION. Decisions above applied in v0.2.14 (pending CI confirmation).
Open: androidTest on real Room DB, scale benchmark, DAO layer, repositories.

27.5 Next steps (in order)
1. Run CI with v0.2.14; confirm 7/7 tests and that the WAL line compiles.
2. Replace app/schemas/.../1.json with the new export from the CI report.
3. Add androidTest and a scale/benchmark plan.
4. Begin DAO layer.

27.6 Change log addition
- v1.15: Scale decisions, FK indexes, WAL, CHECK count reconciled.

30. PACKAGE v0.2.15 - ANDROIDTEST AND SCALE BENCHMARK (STEP A STARTED) - VERSION 1.18
============================================================

Planning update date: 17 November 2026
Package baseline: QuestionBank Android v0.2.15 (no main-source change)

30.1 What was done (execution of section 29, step A)
- Section 29 (v1.17) was verified present in the plan: status corrections, hybrid execution order, scale-driven
  scope additions (Paging 3, image sharding and thumbnails, streaming backup, benchmark gate).
- Added app/src/androidTest: TestDb helper, RoomRuntimeTest (8 tests), ScaleBenchmarkTest.
- Added manual workflow android-instrumented.yml (emulator) and a non-blocking androidTest compile step in the normal workflow.
- STATUS: written, NOT compiled, NOT run. The APIs used (Room 2.8 driver connections) were written without a compiler;
  the first run may need small fixes. Do not mark Phase 2 runtime verification as done until the emulator run passes.

30.2 Offline prototype findings (Python sqlite3, same SQL; not a device result)
- F-1 (design risk, High): FTS sync triggers delete and update FTS rows with
  `DELETE FROM *_question_fts WHERE question_id = ...`, but question_id is an UNINDEXED FTS column, so every edit and delete
  scans the whole FTS table. Measured 18.3 ms per row at 50,000 rows versus 0.019 ms when deleting by rowid; the cost grows
  linearly with row count. At 300,000 rows a single edit would cost on the order of 100 ms on a desktop CPU and more on a phone;
  bulk delete or bulk edit would be very slow. To be confirmed by ScaleBenchmarkTest (section 5 of the test) on the emulator.
  Options (decide after the benchmark; ADR-003; the canonical schema is not changed until then):
    a) Add an integer surrogate column (for example fts_id INTEGER UNIQUE, app-assigned) to the question tables and use it as the FTS rowid.
    b) Use the implicit rowid of the question tables as the FTS rowid (simple, but SQLite may renumber implicit rowids on VACUUM for tables
       without an INTEGER PRIMARY KEY; only safe if VACUUM is never run and if restore keeps rowids - not recommended).
    c) Make question_id an indexed FTS column and delete with an exact-column MATCH (IDs must tokenize as one token).
  Recommendation: (a). Database version is still 1 and nothing is released, so no migration is needed.
- F-2 (search correctness, Medium): a word stored with precomposed য় (U+09DF) is not found by the decomposed query
  (U+09AF U+09BC) and the reverse. Fix: normalize text on write and on query (NFC plus folding of য়, ড়, ঢ়). Maps to Phase 12 "Normalization".
- Bangla tokenization with the current tokenizer (unicode61 plus tokenchars for vowel signs, hasanta, chandrabindu): whole-word and prefix
  searches for conjunct words worked in the prototype. Device confirmation pending (RoomRuntimeTest.banglaWordsAndPrefixesAreSearchable).

30.3 Status
PHASE 1: BUILD GATE PASSED, FOUNDATION INCOMPLETE (DI, navigation/UI, release variant, instrumentation run, version-freeze document).
PHASE 2: PARTIAL FOUNDATION. Schema parity, FK indexes, WAL: verified at compile/JDBC level.
         Runtime tests and benchmark: WRITTEN, NOT RUN.
PHASES 3-17: NOT STARTED.

1. Push v0.2.15; confirm the normal workflow is still green and read build-report/androidtest-compile.log.
2. Run workflow "Android Instrumented Tests and Scale Benchmark" with benchmark_rows=20000; send QuestionBank-instrumented-report.
3. Repeat with 300000 rows; record the numbers in Test_Execution_Report.md.

5. Record the UI toolkit and DI decision (ADR-002); then Step B of section 29.2.
